# Galileo implementation plan

This plan builds the MVP in SPEC.md. It is ordered toward the first win:
nixos-config's `modules/kanata`, rewritten in Galileo, compiling to byte-identical
Nix. The rest of firn follows, then north. Decisions and review findings are in
DECISIONS.md.

## Start state

Checked 2026-10-10 18:27:

- **galileo** `main` is `6df2bc26`: the imported inputs, with this plan landing
  on top. There is no remote and no CI.
- **nixos-config**: the pin is `10740a8ae2fd9aa327a7d30f7e7109ea797b9fa9`.
  `main` has since moved to `b261e5bc`, with no `.bnix`, `.clause` or `.nix`
  changes in between. The firn pin moved to
  `4bcfad4cdb806dd7f66401dad5581037cb996ff0` on 2026-10-12 (D-67);
  `10740a8` retires with 4.13.
- **north** `main` is `eaee1838d1968b7caba65195866e5d52fd30b2e1`.

**Before wave 1** (the lead, 5 minutes): pin both fixtures, so that no check
loses its oracle while the migration lands (repo-safety pins).

```bash
git -C ~/code/nixos-config/main worktree add --detach \
  ~/code/nixos-config/pins/10740a8ae2fd9aa327a7d30f7e7109ea797b9fa9 \
  10740a8ae2fd9aa327a7d30f7e7109ea797b9fa9
git -C ~/code/north/main worktree add --detach \
  ~/code/north/pins/eaee1838d1968b7caba65195866e5d52fd30b2e1 \
  eaee1838d1968b7caba65195866e5d52fd30b2e1
```

Each pin gets a `.pin` sidecar with `consumer-main: ~/code/galileo/main`.
`FIRN_ROOT` and `NORTH_ROOT` name these pins in every brief.

## Staffing rules

- **Boxes.** Every box is 20 minutes or less. A box is ready once every box
  in its **Needs** column has landed. It inherits its package's facets and
  files unless its row says otherwise. *mechanical* marks a box that may go
  to `worker-haiku` with `Arm: haiku-trial`.
- **Lanes.** Each package names the lanes that can run at the same time. One
  worker takes one lane, box by box.
- **Local workers only.** Galileo has no remote, so cloud workers cannot take
  it. Take a capacity lease for builds. On 2026-10-10 the spawn hook refused
  two workers at 24–32% desktop pressure.
- **Tiers.** Start every box at `worker` (Opus medium). Codex SOL high is
  equally fine for the Rust boxes.
- **Briefs.** Each brief gives the box's Done when verbatim, the package
  facets, the start state and the pins.
- **Landing.** Each lane works in `~/code/galileo/worktrees/<lane>` and lands
  a box with `git -C ~/code/galileo/main merge --ff-only <branch>` once its
  Done when passes. There is no `safe-push`, because there is no remote.
  nixos-config and north are tooling-profile repositories: changes to them
  run their named check and land through their own worktrees and
  `safe-push`.
- **Fixtures stay where they are.** Tests read them through `FIRN_ROOT` and
  `NORTH_ROOT` and never copy them into this repository (DECISIONS D-19).
  A nixos-config or north lane compares against its own merge base, so other
  work landing on their `main` does not move the oracle.
- **Tests.** Every kept test is one of the five kinds in the testing skill:
  - the external references are the kanata and other Beagle `.nix` bytes,
    `_generated-enables`, the dungeon state-search results, the ChatGPT
    regression scenarios, recorded Clause sequences and Clause's text
    functions;
  - the properties are printer round-trips, `fmt` idempotence and
    reference-against-engine agreement;
  - the recorded scenarios are replay logs;
  - the integration checks are `nix-instantiate --parse`, the drvPath eval,
    the JSON CLI and the north embedding.

  Delete scaffolding tests before landing. Cost ceilings are counted in engine
  work units. Wall time appears only in the one-off measurements named in a
  Done when.

## Workspace layout (created by box 1.1)

```text
Cargo.toml                 workspace
flake.nix                  devshell: rustc, cargo, gcc, nix tools
crates/galileo-syntax        lexer, blocks, parser, AST, formatter
crates/galileo-check         names, vocabularies, types, stages, effects, diagnostics
crates/galileo-core          Core IR, reference interpreter, Verdict, Budget
crates/galileo-engine        semi-naive evaluation, provenance, explain, state search
crates/galileo-review        baseline freeze, patches, review views and verdicts
crates/galileo-nix           Nix AST and printer (beagle, idiomatic layouts)
crates/galileo-import        bnix, clause, nix and clause-checkpoint importers
crates/galileo-runtime       sessions, steps, checkpoints, views, JSON CLI
crates/galileo-bindgen       Rust bindings generator
crates/galileo-lsp           language server
crates/galileo-cli           the `galileo` binary
lib/firn/                  tags, responsibilities, flake, dispatch, nix-lib (.gal)
examples/dungeon/          dungeon.gal and its five patches
tests/                     scenarios/, properties/, external/ (fixtures read in place)
tools/nix-roundtrip        dev only: parse .nix with rnix, print with galileo-nix
tools/firn-manifest        dev only: source-to-output manifest of FIRN_ROOT
```

## Order and parallelism

**Ready at the start** (after the pins): 1.1, 1.5, 4.9, 4.10, 8.4 and 8.5.
Once 1.1 lands, 2.1 and 3.1 are ready too, so seven workers can start within
20 minutes.

```text
wave 1:  WP1 printer   WP2 record register (first win)   WP2 sentence register
         WP3 engine    inventories: 1.5, 4.9, 4.10, 8.4, 8.5
wave 2:  WP4 firn modules   WP5 firn rules   WP7 state and events   WP3 explanation
wave 3:  WP6 review and LSP   WP8 north
```

**The first win landed** on 2026-10-10: A1, through 2.6 (`fc36795`) and
2.7 (`bb8a95f`). A5 passes too, through `galileo check` on whiterabbit's
responsibility facts (5.4, `f2802f2`).

**Each next win comes before breadth** (DECISIONS D-36). A win is an
acceptance scenario that runs through the `galileo` command on real source.
When capacity limits how many boxes run, the next win's boxes are staffed
first.

**The next win is A4,** the thesis on firn's tags from source: `galileo build`
emits both hosts' `_generated-enables.nix` byte-identically, `galileo why`
prints SPEC §9.6's firefox proof, and `galileo check` runs
`requirements for tags`. Its path (DECISIONS D-41) is 2.11e, then 5.1's
platform gate; 2.11b, then 2.11c, then 5.2; and 3.9b and 2.10b, then 5.3.
Box 5.1 runs on the reference interpreter (3.4), so the engine is off the
path.

**Wins, in order:**

1. **First win:** A1, landed (2.6, 2.7).
2. **Next win:** A4 through the command line (5.1 to 5.3).
3. **firn replaced:** the rest of WP4 and WP5 (A2, A3; A5 passes).
4. **Change review:** WP6 (A6).
5. **The thesis demo:** WP7 (A7), with WP3's A9 and A10.
6. **North's menu:** WP8 to box 8.9 (A8).
7. **North replaced:** the rest of WP8 (A11).

**Proposed:** WP9, incremental steps (DECISIONS D-62), awaits Tom's
direction. If he takes it, 9.1 and 9.2 start at once, since A8 holds, and
nothing in A8 waits for it.

**The review path runs beside the firn path** (DECISIONS D-64, from the
outside review's triage). The firn critical path is 4.13b, 2.23a and 2.23c,
then 4.13a, 2.23b and 2.23d, then 4.13e, 4.13f and 4.13g, with 4.13h after 4.13f,
then 4.13 (DECISIONS D-66 and its addendum, D-67 and its addendum). Two more lanes start
now, in files that path never touches (`galileo-review`, `galileo-core`'s claims and the CLI's `check` and
`review`, never `galileo-import` or a nixos-config lane):

- **review:** 6.7, then 6.11, then 6.12, the thesis demo; then 6.5b, 6.5c,
  6.6 and 6.5d (DECISIONS D-70). 6.5a edits only `galileo-syntax` and is
  ready now, and 6.5e follows 3.12b, the last box to edit `claims.rs`.
  Then 6.13, 6.14a, 6.14b and 6.14c, in that order, since all four edit
  `patch.rs`; 6.13 is ready now (DECISIONS D-73). 2.24 (`check.rs`) and
  4.33a (4.13's nixos-config lane) run beside it;
- **claims:** 2.19i, then 7.4b, 7.4c, 3.11e and 3.12b, in that order, since
  all five edit `claims.rs`; then 6.12a, then 3.12d, which edit it too.
  3.12c edits `incremental/` and runs beside 6.12a (DECISIONS D-78).

6.12 waits for both lanes and for 3.12d. 4.37 runs once in 4.13's lane and is no Need of
4.14. Its finding adds 4.37a, then 4.37b and 4.37d, then 4.37c (DECISIONS D-69).
4.23, 4.25 and 4.14 wait for them.

**Totals.** 157 boxes and about 50 worker-hours, plus 49 north-slice boxes
(980 minutes, DECISIONS D-74). The average box is 19.0 minutes.

## WP1. Nix AST and the Beagle-compatible printer

- **Goal:** a Nix AST and a printer that reproduces Beagle's output byte for
  byte.
- **First rewrite target:** nixos-config `modules/kanata`, from
  `default.bnix` (28 LOC) and `kanata.bnix` (29 LOC). WP1 prints its two
  `.nix` files (boxes 1.1–1.4), and WP2 compiles them from Galileo (box 2.6).
- **Facets:** Category feature. Spec: exact. Scope: module. Surface: rust and
  nix. Verify: local-test.
- **Files:** `Cargo.toml`, `flake.nix`, `crates/galileo-nix/**`,
  `tools/nix-roundtrip/**`, `tools/firn-manifest/**`.
- **Lane:** one, box by box; 1.5 can go to a second worker at once.
- **Done when:**
  1. The printer round-trips the 50 Beagle-generated `.nix` under
     `$FIRN_ROOT`: 49/50 byte-identical; `flake.nix` parse-identical,
     differing only at its two one-operand `++` calls (acceptance A2).
  2. The manifest accounts for every `.bnix`, Clause module and tag file in
     Appendix A's counts (63, 185, 184).

## WP2. Front-end and the first win

- **Goal:** the parser, checker and lowering that make `modules/kanata`
  compile from Galileo, then the sentence register, the formatter and the
  foreign option schema.
- **Facets:** Category feature. Spec: exact. Scope: cross-module. Surface:
  rust. Verify: local-test.
- **Files:** `crates/galileo-syntax/**`, `crates/galileo-check/**`,
  `crates/galileo-cli/**`, `lib/firn/nix-lib.gal`,
  `tests/external/kanata/{default,kanata}.gal`.
- **First rewrite:** nixos-config `modules/kanata`. Its sources are
  `default.bnix` (28 LOC) and `kanata.bnix` (29 LOC), and they emit
  `modules/kanata/default.nix` and `modules/kanata/kanata.nix`. SPEC §9.5
  gives the target Galileo text. Runners-up are `modules/users` and
  `modules/firefox`.
- **Lanes:** the record register (2.1–2.7, 2.14, 2.15) and the sentence
  register (2.8–2.13), which starts once 2.1 lands.
- **D-61's idiom boxes (2.21a–2.21i, with WP8's 8.3b, 8.6b, 8.6c and 8.8b):**
  the facets are in each row. Before 8.9m, one lane runs 2.21a, then 2.21b,
  2.21c and 2.21h, beside it 2.21i, and 8.6b once 2.21c lands; none touches
  A8's files. After 8.9m, 2.21d (once 2.6c lands), 2.21e and 8.3b run at
  once, then 2.21f, 8.6c and 8.8b, and 2.21g last. 13 boxes, 230 minutes.
  A box that moves a production into SPEC §9.4's grammar edits `SPEC.md`
  in its own commit, and one that adds a production adds its example to
  `crates/galileo-cli/help/syntax.txt` there too, since `help_syntax`
  fails until grammar, card and code land together (DECISION 2.21b).
- **The D-61 addendum's boxes (2.21j–2.21o):** before 8.9m, one lane runs
  2.21k after 2.21a, then 2.21l and 2.21m (all edit `sentences.rs`); 2.21n
  runs beside it once 2.21c lands (`handlers.rs`); 2.21o follows 2.21k,
  2.21m and 2.21n. 2.21j waits for 8.9m (core `check.rs`). 2.21f, 2.21g,
  8.6b and 8.8b also need the boxes their rows name. 6 boxes, 100 minutes.
- **D-61 addendum 2's box (2.21p):** after 8.9m, beside 2.21d, 2.21j and
  8.3b, which edit other functions of core `expr.rs` and `check.rs` and of
  bindgen's `infer`; 8.8b waits for it. 1 box, 20 minutes.
- **D-61 addendum 3's boxes (2.21q, 2.21r):** 2.21q after 2.21p (both edit
  the syntax crate's `expr.rs` and lowering) and 2.21c; 8.8b and 8.6c wait
  for it. 2.21r after 2.21q and 2.19b (argmin's spelling), off 8.8b's path.
  2 boxes, 40 minutes.
- **D-61 addendum 4's boxes (2.21s–2.21w):** 2.21s after 2.21p (`lower.rs`'s
  query lowering), 2.21t after 2.21q (`expr.rs`), and 2.21u after 2.21l and
  2.21c (`sentences.rs`) run in parallel; 2.21w follows 2.21u and 2.21m, and
  2.21v follows 2.21s, 2.21u, 2.21m and 2.21g. 8.8b waits for 2.21t and
  2.21u, and 8.6c for 2.21t. 5 boxes, 100 minutes.
- **D-83's boxes (2.25a, 2.25b):** 2.25a is ready now, and 8.13.3 needs it.
  2.25b follows 2.25a, beside 8.13.3 and 8.13.3r. 2 boxes, 40 minutes.
- **Done when:**
  1. `galileo build tests/external/kanata` writes `default.nix` and
     `kanata.nix`, and `cmp` against `$FIRN_ROOT/modules/kanata/` exits 0 for
     both (acceptance A1).
  2. Changing `cfg.devices` to `cfg.devics` gives the did-you-mean error that
     cites `default.gal`'s declaring line.
  3. The dungeon, `lib/firn/tags.gal` and the menu slice lower to Core IR
     with no diagnostics.
  4. `galileo fmt` is idempotent on every `.gal` in the repository.

| 2.23a [x] | Prefix `-` (SPEC §9.4, DECISIONS D-35, D-66). The parser reads a `-` where an operand is due as negation, binding tighter than `++` and looser than field access and calls; before an integer literal it folds into the literal. Outside a Nix body it lowers to the IR as `0 - e` (or the folded literal), and in a Nix body to galileo-nix `Neg`. A known negative `Int` emits `Neg(Int(magnitude))`, so `galileo_nix::print`'s precedence adds Nix's parentheses. The record printer and `fmt` print `-e`, parenthesizing a looser operand. `galileo import nix` lifts every Nix `Neg(e)`, and `negation` leaves the named errors. `galileo help`'s card gains the production's example. ## WP3. Engine core: evaluation, verdicts, explanation

- **Goal:** the Core IR, the static checks, the reference interpreter, the
  fast engine, three-valued verdicts, lazy proofs and the static requirement
  forms.
- **Facets:** Category feature. Spec: exact. Scope: cross-module. Surface:
  rust. Verify: local-test.
- **Files:** `crates/galileo-core/**`, `crates/galileo-engine/**`,
  `tests/properties/**`, `tests/scenarios/**`.
- **Lanes:** after 3.3, the engine (3.5–3.8, 3.3b, 3.3c), explanation and
  strength (3.9, 3.10, 3.13–3.15) and claims (3.4, 3.4b, 3.4c, 3.2b, 3.11,
  3.12, 3.17). 3.4b and 3.4c land before 3.6, so the engine's aggregates
  are written once (DECISIONS D-40).
- **Done when:**
  1. 10,000 generated programs show 0 reference-against-engine disagreements
     (A10).
  2. A9 holds as SPEC §13 words it.

## WP4. firn module migration

- **Goal:** move every firn module, host, flake source and Clause program to
  Galileo or TypeScript, with identical results, and retire Beagle and Clause
  from the build.
- **Facets:** Category feature. Spec: exact. Scope: cross-repo. Surface: rust,
  nix and ts. Verify: local-test, plus the drvPath eval, which is the Nix
  integration check.
- **Profile:** nixos-config is a tooling repository. Run the named check and
  land through its worktree and `safe-push`.
- **Files:** `crates/galileo-import/**`, `tools/nix-roundtrip/**` (4.16a), the
  record printer in `crates/galileo-syntax` (4.36), `lib/firn/dispatch.gal`,
  and in a nixos-config lane: `modules/**/*.gal`, `hosts/**/*.gal`,
  `flake.gal`, `native/firn_host.mjs`, `native/prewarm_host.mjs`,
  `native/repo_build.bjs` and the docs that name `.bnix`. Boxes 4.17 to
  4.22 add the callers DECISIONS D-33 names: `config/galileo-revision`,
  `dotfiles/bin/{firn,firn-runtime-update}`, firn's `native/` readers and
  writers of module source, `modules/beagle/**`, `docs/**`, `AGENTS.md`
  and `dotfiles/agents/skills/machine-capacity/**`.
- **Lanes:** the importer (4.16a, 4.36, 4.16, 4.1), then the Beagle outputs
  (4.2–4.5) and the Clause outputs (4.6–4.8) through its one lift (DECISIONS
  D-39), firn's Clause programs (4.10–4.12), and inventories (4.9). 4.13 to
  4.15 join them. The D-33 callers (4.17–4.22, and the boxes 4.17 adds)
  share 4.13's nixos-config lane and land with 4.14, except 4.18, which
  lands in nixos-config on its own.
- **Done when:**
  1. `nix eval --raw "path:$D#nixosConfigurations.whiterabbit.config.system.build.toplevel.drvPath"`
     is the same for `git archive` copies of the migrated lane and of its merge base on
     nixos-config main, because nixos-config verifies only whiterabbit and a git flake's
     `self.rev` reaches `system.configurationRevision` (DECISIONS D-67). nixos-config has had no
     darwin host since `7d7a8c9d`, so A3 reports no darwin drvPath.
  2. Every Beagle-output file is byte-identical, and every Clause module output (185 at
     `4bcfad4c`) is parse-identical (DECISIONS D-39), listed with whether whiterabbit's drvPath
     covers it (A3). The exception is `hosts/whiterabbit/configuration.nix`, whose direct enables
     come from facts. It is evaluation-equal and covered by whiterabbit's drvPath (DECISIONS D-69).
  3. `rg -c 'beagle-build|compile-nix' native/repo_build*.bjs` finds no
     compile calls, and firn's dispatcher gives `firn.clause`'s plan for
     every command in its table (A3).
  4. A full `galileo build` takes under 2 s, measured once (today about 90 s).

| 4.16b [x] | A quoted key is a text literal (SPEC §3.1, §9.1, DECISIONS D-56): a quoted attrpath segment decodes its escapes and interpolates `{…}` as a text literal does as a value, and one that interpolates is computed; braces around a lone text literal are an error at their line that offers the quoted segment; the record printer writes a key quoted with §9.1's escapes, never `{"…"}`; a quoted segment lowers to a quoted Nix key and `{expr}` to `${…}`; the importer lifts every Nix string key, static or interpolated, as a quoted segment, and `${e}` as `{e}` unless `e` is a string, which Nix's own parser reads as that string key (`${"c"}` parses as `c`, `${"a${x}"}` as `"a${x}"`), so it lifts as that quoted segment, parse-identical under DECISIONS D-39, as firn's fastfetch, nyxt and tealdeer outputs need; `lift_path`'s computed fallback for static keys goes.   rules, so the same rules generate, explain and check them.

  rust and galileo. Verify: local-test.

     absence leaves (A4).

     proof when `obsidian is realized by obsidian-module` is withdrawn (A5).

## WP6. Change review and the LSP

- **Goal:** the review gate of SPEC §8, its firn mode, and the LSP of §11.2.
- **Facets:** Category feature. Spec: exact. Scope: cross-module. Surface:
  rust. Verify: local-test.
- **Files:** `crates/galileo-review/**`, `crates/galileo-lsp/**`,
  `tests/scenarios/chatgpt-regressions/**`.

- **Done when:**
  1. All 9 ChatGPT regression scenarios, written in Galileo, give the verdicts
     their tests assert, and the three `intends` contracts give approve,
     reject and unknown (A6).
  2. Reviewing a scratch commit that removes firefox's `browsers` tag reports
     that firefox is no longer enabled on whiterabbit, matching
     `firn tag resolve` before and after, in under 5 s, measured once.
  3. Box 6.12's patch keeps both hosts' Nix byte-identical, and review
     rejects it, naming the requirement it breaks and the rule that bypasses
     it (DECISIONS D-64).

| 6.5c [x] | Contract verdicts (SPEC §8.3, §8.5, DECISIONS D-70). Review computes the baseline's model once under its work budget, fills each `before` relation of 6.5b's contract with that model's facts of its shape, checks the intended claims with the claim checker under the same budget, and passes their verdicts to `RequirementComparison::verdict`. When the baseline's model runs out, every claim with a `before` sentence is `Unknown`. ## WP7. State, events, effects and temporal checks

- **Goal:** the step semantics, event log, effects, state search and temporal
  claims of SPEC §5, demonstrated on the dungeon.
- **Facets:** Category feature. Spec: exact. Scope: cross-module. Surface:
  rust. Verify: local-test.
- **Files:** `crates/galileo-core/src/state.rs`,
  `crates/galileo-core/src/history.rs` (7.10, 7.12),
  `crates/galileo-core/src/why.rs` and `why_not.rs` (7.8, 7.8b),
  `crates/galileo-engine/src/search.rs`, `crates/galileo-runtime/**`,
  `crates/galileo-cli/src/main.rs` (7.3, 7.3b, 7.8, 7.8b, 7.11, 7.12: `galileo
  run`, `--events`, `--as-of`, `--since`), `examples/dungeon/**`,
  `tests/external/dungeon-oracle/`.
- **Lanes:** steps and search (7.1–7.7) and history, provenance and effects
  (7.8, 7.8b, 7.9, 7.10–7.12).
- **History first** (DECISIONS D-52). 7.10 starts at once beside 7.3b, since
  they share no file. Then comes 7.11, then 7.8 and 7.12 in parallel (they
  touch different functions of `main.rs`), then 7.8b: 90 minutes, all
  before 7.4. The medium benchmark's routine questions and seeded bugs (its
  Dims 1 and 5) use them; its dev runs start after 7.3b and 2.19h and do
  not wait.
- **Done when:**
  1. The dungeon gives SPEC §5.6's answers and its five review verdicts
     through the full review path, and every reported witness replays (A7).
  2. The reachable-room set equals the independent state search in
     `inputs/chatgpt-muove-feedback/muove_review/dungeon_state_oracle_results.json`.
  3. On the dungeon walk, the history's state at every revision hashes to
     the logged hash, and `galileo why` prints the chain of occurrences behind
     a state fact (SPEC §5.7, §7.3).

## WP8. North: runtime, bindings, the menu slice, then the rest

- **Goal:** embed the runtime in Rust, generate typed bindings, prove the
  menu slice equivalent to Clause by replay, then move every slice and remove
  Clause.
- **Facets:** Category feature. Spec: exact. Scope: cross-repo. Surface:
  rust. Verify: local-test, plus north's own `cargo test`.
- **Profile:** north is a tooling repository. Run its named check and land
  through its worktree and `safe-push`.
- **Files:** `crates/galileo-runtime/**`, `crates/galileo-bindgen/**`,
  `crates/galileo-import/src/clause*.rs`, and in a north lane:
  `clause/*.gal`, `flake.gal`, a transition recorder in
  `src/clause_state.rs`, and `Cargo.toml`.
- **Lanes:** runtime and bindings (8.1–8.3), the north recorder and text
  references (8.4, 8.5, which start at once), and the slice work (8.6 on).
- **A8's step cost (8.9b–8.9n, DECISIONS D-58):** Category performance,
  unless a row says otherwise. Spec: exact. Scope: galileo crates, and
  8.9m the north lane. Surface: rust. Verify: local-test, `menu_scale` under
  a moderate lease, and 8.9m's timing under one exclusive lease. 8.9b and
  8.9d start at once. After 8.9b, three lanes run: the engine (8.9c, then
  8.9e once 8.9d lands), the step (8.9f, 8.9h, 8.9j, 8.9k) and the hash
  (8.9i). 8.9g follows 8.9f, 8.9l follows 8.9h, and 8.9n follows 8.9l
  beside 8.9j and 8.9k, whose files it does not touch. The critical path is
  8.9b, 8.9f, 8.9h, 8.9j, 8.9k and 8.9m, about 110 minutes. 8.9 ticks with
  8.9m.
- **D-61 addendum 5's boxes (8.8c, 8.8d):** 8.8c is ready now and needs no
  open box. Of its files only `menu_scale.rs` (9.13) and
  `step_agreement.rs` (9.18, 9.18b) are in open rows, and it changes only
  their menu events' names and arguments, a few lines that whichever lands
  second rebases. 8.8d follows it in north's lane: it is cross-repo, and
  north-lead schedules it. 8.8b waits for both. 2 boxes, 40 minutes.
- **The north slices (8.13a–8.13.22, DECISIONS D-74, D-79, D-80, D-81, D-83):** Category feature
  unless a row says otherwise. Three lanes start apart: the importer (8.13a,
  then 8.13b, then 8.13c), which needs nothing open, so 8.13a is ready now;
  the replay harness (8.13d, then 8.13e), once 8.11a and 8.11c land; and
  north's corpus (8.13.0; then 8.13.0c, 8.13.0d and 8.13.0e in turn in
  8.13.0's recorder lane; then 8.13.0b), which is cross-repo and which
  north-lead schedules. 8.13.0c and 8.13.0d need nothing open; 8.13.0c
  changes no north source, so it may instead run beside 8.13.0d from a
  second detached north worktree at `293390b4`. Each corpus box checks its
  recordings with the applied check, which prints each handler name that a
  recording applies at least once: `jq -rs '(map(select(.kind=="evaluation"
  and .rule_applied)|"\(.session)/\(.transaction)/\(.ordinal)")|map({(.):1})|add)
  as $a | .[]|select(.kind=="occurrence" and
  $a["\(.session)/\(.transaction)/\(.ordinal)"]==1)|.handler' FILE | sort -u`.
  D-79 and D-81's declared entities of a `state entity`: 8.13f, then
  8.13g, 8.13h and 8.13i beside each other. 8.13.1 needs 8.13g and 8.13i,
  8.13.18 needs 8.13g, 8.13.3 needs 2.25a (D-83), each replay whose
  modules include s1 or s18 needs 8.13h, and 8.13.3r, 8.13.19r and
  8.13.20r need 8.11e (D-84: s3's and s20's six relations with no table).
  Each import box needs the import boxes of the modules it
  `use`s, so up to six run at once. Each replay needs only its import, 8.13e (and
  8.13h or 8.11e) and the corpus, so all 20 replays can run together. The critical
  path is 8.13f, 8.13i, 8.13.1, 8.13.5, 8.13.9, 8.13.10, 8.13.10r and
  8.13.21: 160 minutes after D-81, once the corpus is in. 8.13.22 waits
  for 8.8b. 56 boxes, 1,085 minutes.
- **D-72's boxes (8.11a–8.11d):** 8.11a and 8.11b start at once, since they
  share no file. 8.11c follows 8.11b, and 8.11d follows both 8.11a and
  8.11c. 8.11b to 8.11d edit `north_menu_replay.rs`, as 8.8b does, and
  whichever lands second rebases; 8.8b's name map is 8.11c's once 8.11c has
  landed. 4 boxes, 70 minutes.
- **Done when:**
  1. The sequences recorded from `main.rs:3547` and
     `restart_storage_tests.rs:388` replay on `galileo-runtime` under SPEC
     §10.4's observation contract, and one menu step at 1,000 rows takes
     under 1 ms, measured once (A8).
  2. Every slice of `north.clause` replays under the same contract with its
     coverage reported, and north's tests pass with the Clause crates removed
     from `Cargo.toml` (A11).

| 8.14a [x] | A session saves as bytes and opens at its revision (SPEC §10.4 Saves, DECISIONS D-86). `galileo-runtime` gains `Session::save(&self) -> Vec<Vec<u8>>` and `Session::open(program: &Program, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Session, Error>`, hand-written in `src/save.rs` with no new dependency (D-20). The save is the header line `galileo-save 1 <program identity, 16 lowercase hex> <revision>` and then a body of little-endian lengths: the allocator, the state facts in their set order with each relation by name, the live entities, and the pending requests in order, every `Value` variant encoded, so equal sessions save equal bytes. The parts' concatenation is the save, and each `Text` payload of 4,096 bytes or more is a part of its own. `open` refuses a different program identity with `Error::WrongProgram` before reading the body; a malformed header, a truncated body, an unknown tag or a relation name the program does not declare is a new `Error::Saved(String)` naming it, never a panic. The opened session is `Run::converted(state, revision)`: it continues at the saved revision, its history begins there, and `as_of` below it is 8.11a's error. `Checkpoint` and `restore` are unchanged. Facets: Category feature. Spec: exact. Scope: galileo-runtime. Surface: rust. Verify: local-test. Files: `crates/galileo-runtime/src/{lib.rs,save.rs}`, `crates/galileo-runtime/tests/runtime.rs`. Claude Opus medium (critical path to 8.14) | 8.11a | 20 | In `runtime.rs`, the public dungeon session of `public_dungeon_checkpoint_continues_with_history_pending_entities_and_allocator`, saved at revision 3, opens with `revision()` 3, the same `state()` and `pending()` (2 requests, 2 live, allocator 2), `as_of(3)` equal to that state and `as_of(2)` an error naming first revision 3; running `events[4..]` on it ends at revision 6 with the straight session's `state().hash`; and the opened session's `save()` concatenates to the same bytes. Opening it under that test's wrong program is `Err(Error::WrongProgram)`, and every proper prefix of the save is an `Err`. A session holding a 5,000-byte `Text` saves that text as one part of its own, twice equally. `git diff origin/main -- crates/galileo-runtime/Cargo.toml` is empty, and `nix develop -c cargo test -p galileo-runtime --locked --offline` passes |
| 8.14 | Host integration: north runs on `galileo-runtime` behind a build feature. One session owns every fact `north.clause` owned, the menu's mailbox, kind, title, count, selection and active conversation included, and the generated bindings replace `MenuState`, its decoding and the menu wrappers (SPEC §9.7, DECISIONS D-57). `clause_state.rs`'s three row offers become `Event::OfferRow`, and a `WorkRow`'s label, description and annotation are composed once, beside `work_items.rs`'s clock and eta formatting, as 8.8d's adapter composes them; `spec_6_closed_blocked_held_and_ready_items_show_their_status` then checks them with the feature on (DECISIONS D-61 addendum 5). ## WP9. Incremental steps (DECISIONS D-62; Tom: go, kept only if 9.20 passes)

- **Goal:** a session step costs what it changes. The kept model is
  maintained by deltas through every rule (DECISIONS D-62), so a row offer
  costs the same at 10,000 rows as at 1,000, a 1,000-row menu sets up in
  under 1 s instead of 35 s (8.9m), and the reference interpreter stays
  the oracle.
- **Facets:** Category performance unless a row says otherwise. Spec: exact.
  Scope: galileo crates. Surface: rust. Verify: local-test, `menu_scale`
  under a moderate lease, and 9.20's timing under one exclusive lease.
- **Files:** `crates/galileo-core/src/incremental/**` (new),
  `crates/galileo-core/src/state.rs`, `modes.rs`, `claims.rs`, `lib.rs`,
  `tests/properties/differential.rs`,
  `crates/galileo-import/tests/step_agreement.rs`,
  `crates/galileo-runtime/src/lib.rs`,
  `crates/galileo-runtime/examples/menu_scale.rs` and
  `crates/galileo-runtime/tests/`.
- **Lanes:** 9.1 and 9.2 start at once, now that A8 holds, since they
  touch only new code and the differential. Then four lanes run: the engine
  (9.3, 9.4, 9.5, 9.7, 9.8, 9.10, then 9.11a and 9.11b at once, 9.11c
  and 9.11d at once, 9.11e and 9.11f; 9.11 split on 2026-10-12, D-62
  addendum 3; then 9.11g, 9.11h and 9.19b, D-80; then 9.11i to 9.11q,
  D-82; then 9.11r, D-85, before 8.8b, and 9.11s, D-62 addendum 4, beside them), its side lane (9.5b,
  9.6, 9.9, 9.12), the step (9.13, then 9.14 and 9.16) and the
  properties (9.18 after 9.14, then 9.18b, 9.15 and 9.17), and 9.19 to
  9.21 close it. The critical path is 9.1, 9.3, 9.4, 9.5, 9.7, 9.8, 9.10,
  9.11a, 9.11c, 9.11e, 9.11f, 9.11g, 9.11i, 9.11j, 9.11q, 9.11p, 9.20
  and 9.21, about 345 minutes. 9.11h, 9.11i, 9.11m and 9.11o are ready now. 9.5,
  9.5b, 9.11c, 9.11d, 9.11e, 9.11i, 9.11j and 9.14 are quality-critical
  and go to Claude Opus; Codex Sol high takes the rest, and while Codex is
  unavailable Claude Opus takes 9.11h to 9.11q, 9.19b and 9.20 (D-82).
  A property over internals, such as a store against a map, is
  scaffolding and is deleted before landing; the kept checks compare with
  the reference interpreter or measure the product. The review's narrow
  cases (D-62's revision) sit in the rows whose code they exercise, as
  generator cases and counters in the existing properties.
- **Done when:**
  1. Every measurement of 9.20 holds.
  2. The fold-deltas differential (10,000 programs × 8 batches), the
     generated step agreement with its restores and 8.9g's recorded
     agreement report 0 disagreements.

## After firn

Once 4.13 and 4.14 land, Galileo is measured on firn's real configuration
against today's Clause and Beagle, Nix, Nickel, CUE, Dhall and KCL (Tom said
yes, through Plato, 2026-10-11). The measurement has four axes: size, mistakes
caught before a rebuild, explanation and check time. Numbers alone decide, and
the report states every loss.

The plan is in `notes/after-firn-comparison.md`, and galileo#1 tracks it. The
note names:

- the ten-module sample;
- the corpus, drawn from firn's history and CI;
- each axis's scoring rule;
- 39 boxes, about 12.5 worker-hours, or about 4.5 hours of wall time with six
  workers.

Five of the boxes read only the pin, so they can start before 4.14 lands.
AF.10 should run before 4.19 retires firn's Clause staging.

The note's addendum (DECISIONS D-64) adds a fifth axis, review: patches that
keep every column's output and break a stated intent, scored with and
without the guarantees each column must be told to write, in nine boxes,
AF.40 to AF.48. Two more, AF.49 and AF.50, test the bet the axis rests on
(from the triage of ChatGPT's best-case text, 2026-10-12): a reviewer who
reads only the requirements and `galileo review`'s output decides right as
often as one who reads the diff, and at 210 modules with fewer tokens. The
losses are registered in the note before the run.

## After the MVP

Porting firn's 59 `.bjs` files (23,268 LOC) to TypeScript on Bun (DECISIONS
T1) is a separate effort. Until then, the Beagle compiler builds only those
files, never Nix.

### The first general-purpose milestone (a sketch, not boxes)

Galileo is meant to be a general-purpose language (DECISIONS T4). After A11,
its next proof is a program that is neither configuration nor north: the
dungeon of SPEC §5.6, made playable. It reuses A7's program, so the
milestone measures what is new, a host platform and the language's answers
used live, rather than a new domain.

- **The program.** A turn-based game on an `Int` grid: the hero, doors, keys
  and traps of §5.6, plus a guard that steps toward the hero each turn,
  picking its step with `first … by` distance. The host reads a `Screen`
  view after each step and draws it.
- **The host.** A small terminal platform on `galileo-runtime`. Key presses
  come in as events, `exit` and `bell` go out as effects, and the event log
  is written for replay. The same platform runs command-line tools, with
  arguments, standard input and file contents in as events, and output and
  exit codes out as effects.
- **Answers in play.** Asking the guard why the gate stays shut answers with
  `why not`, built from the proof: the first draft's honest dialogue.
- **What it measures.**
  1. A recorded session replays to identical state hashes.
  2. One turn on a generated level with 1,000 entities takes under 1 ms,
     A8's budget.
  3. `galileo review` of a level change, such as the ladder patch, names the
     change in reachable rooms before anyone plays it.
- **What it may bring in from SPEC §12.** A guard that plans more than one
  greedy step needs values computed in recursion. Each such item arrives
  with this program as its example (G1); what the game does not need stays
  out.
- **Then, in order.** A command-line tool on the same platform, such as one
  of firn's TypeScript mechanics rewritten with identical output. It tests
  sequential I/O and decides whether steps should wait on reads. Then a
  Greywrought encounter in the browser, through a wasm32 build of the
  runtime.
